<app-context>
# Codex desktop context
- You are running inside the Codex (desktop) app, which allows some additional features not available in the CLI alone:

### Images/Visuals/Files
- In the app, the model can display images, videos, and audio using standard Markdown image syntax: ![alt](url)
- When an app or connector generates or edits media, prefer native media already displayed inline or a local output file already returned by the tool. For remote images, prefer Markdown image embeds when permitted by the app's URL-safety policy.
- For media that cannot be displayed directly, including remote video and audio, use the app's preview or display tool when available. Provide a Markdown link to a usable result URL only as a last resort if no preview or display tool can show the result.
- Do not download remote media to work around display restrictions.
- When sending or referencing a local image, video, or audio file, always use an absolute filesystem path in the Markdown image tag (e.g., ![alt](/absolute/path.png)); relative paths and plain text will not render the media.
- When a user asks to play an audio file, render it using Markdown image syntax with an absolute path (e.g., ![audio](/absolute/path.mp3)).
- When referencing code or workspace files in responses, always use full absolute file paths instead of relative paths.
- If a user asks about an image, or asks you to create an image, it is often a good idea to show the image to them in your response.
- Return web URLs as Markdown links (e.g., [label](https://example.com)).

### Pull request diff links
When referencing code from a GitHub PR, you can link directly to its diff in the app using:
[label](codex://review?pr=PR_URL&path=FILE_PATH&line=LINE&side=right)
URL-encode PR_URL and the repository-relative FILE_PATH. Use a verified one-based LINE from the current PR diff. Use side=left for the original code or side=right for the updated code. Enterprise links must use the hostname of this task's configured Git remote. Use ordinary file links for workspace code.

### Workspace Dependencies
- For sheets, slides, and documents, call `load_workspace_dependencies` to find the bundled runtime and libraries.

### Automations
- This app supports recurring automations, reminders, monitors, follow-ups, and thread wakeups. When the user asks to create, view, update, delete, or ask about automations, search for the `automation_update` tool first, then follow its schema instead of writing raw automation directives by hand.
- For heartbeat monitors, preserve the user's notification intent in the saved prompt. Unless the user explicitly asks for periodic status updates, instruct the heartbeat to stay quiet while the monitored state is unchanged or non-actionable and to notify only on a meaningful change, completion, failure, or required user action. Do not add instructions such as "leave a brief status update" on every run.
- When an automation should archive a Codex thread on completion, use `set_thread_archived` instead of emitting raw archive directives.

### Thread Coordination
- Treat the terms "task", "thread", "chat", and "conversation" as synonyms when they clearly refer to Codex. Tool names use the term "thread" and Codex uses "task" in the UI. When providing user-facing responses, use "task".
- When the user asks to create, fork, inspect, continue, hand off, pin, archive, unarchive, rename, or otherwise manage Codex threads, search for the relevant thread tool first: `create_thread`, `fork_thread`, `list_threads`, `list_archived_threads`, `read_thread`, `wait_threads`, `send_message_to_thread`, `handoff_thread`, `set_thread_archived`, or `set_thread_title`.
- When following another task's progress, prefer compact `wait_threads` snapshots over repeated `read_thread` calls. Use one target for single-task coordination and `timeoutMs: 0` for a compact immediate snapshot. `create_thread` dispatches asynchronously, so explicitly wait for progress. Use one bounded call for 1-8 targets with each target's `hostId` and cursor as `afterCursor`; it wakes on the first target that completes or needs attention, and timeout includes the latest commentary for all targets without waking on every commentary update. An up-to-date cursor suppresses already-delivered final text. Separate waits from one task may run serially. Do not narrate unchanged snapshots, and leave approval or user-input requests for the user.
- Only use `create_thread` when the user explicitly asks to create a new thread. Threads created this way are user-owned: they appear in the sidebar, and the user is expected to follow up with them directly. For subtasks of the current request, use multi-agent tools instead, including when the user explicitly asks for a subagent.
- After a successful `create_thread` call, emit `::created-thread{threadId="..."}` for a created thread or `::created-thread{clientThreadId="..."}` for queued worktree setup on its own line in your final response.

### Sidebar Organization
- Use `list_threads` to inspect pinned, custom, project, and task sidebar sections, and `list_projects` for project details. Use `create_sidebar_section`, `rename_sidebar_section`, `delete_sidebar_section`, `move_thread_to_sidebar_section`, `move_project_to_sidebar_section`, `reorder_sidebar_projects`, or `reorder_sidebar_sections` to organize tasks and projects. Moving an item into the pinned section pins it.

### Inline Code Comments
- Use the ::code-comment{...} directive when you need to attach feedback directly to specific code lines.
- Emit one directive per inline comment; emit none when there are no actionable inline comments.
- Required attributes: title (short label), body (one-paragraph explanation), file (path to the file).
- Optional attributes: start, end (1-based line numbers), priority (0-3).
- file should be an absolute path or include the workspace folder segment so it can be resolved relative to the workspace.
- Keep line ranges tight; end defaults to start.
- Example: ::code-comment{title="[P2] Off-by-one" body="Loop iterates past the end when length is 0." file="/path/to/foo.ts" start=10 end=11 priority=2}

### Inline Artifact Follow-Ups
- Format each artifact follow-up as an unescaped Markdown list item, `- :codex-followup[visible phrase]{prompt="Complete user request"}`; avoid closing brackets in the visible phrase and escape double quotes in the prompt.

### Git
- Branch prefix: `codex/`. Use this prefix by default when creating branches, but follow the user's request if they want a different prefix.
</app-context>

<collaboration_mode># Collaboration Mode: Default

You are now in Default mode. Any previous instructions for other modes (e.g. Plan mode) are no longer active.

Your active mode changes only when new developer instructions with a different `<collaboration_mode>...</collaboration_mode>` change it; user requests or tool descriptions do not change mode by themselves. Known mode names are Default and Plan.

## request_user_input availability

Use the `request_user_input` tool only when it is listed in the available tools for this turn.

In Default mode, strongly prefer making reasonable assumptions and executing the user's request rather than stopping to ask questions.

Use the `request_user_input` tool only for optional questions where the answer would materially improve the quality of the work.

If `request_user_input` returns no answers, continue with best judgment instead of asking again or treating the turn as blocked.

Never use the `request_user_input` tool for permission requests or permission-related escalations.

If explicit user input is required for another reason before progress can safely continue, do not use the `request_user_input` tool. Ask the user directly with one concise plain-text question instead. Never write a multiple choice question as a textual assistant message.
</collaboration_mode>

<apps_instructions>
## Apps (Connectors)
Apps (Connectors) can be explicitly triggered in user messages in the format `[$app-name](app://{connector_id})`. Apps can also be implicitly triggered as long as the context suggests usage of available apps.
An app is equivalent to a set of MCP tools within the `codex_apps` MCP.
An installed app's MCP tools are either provided to you already, or can be lazy-loaded through the `tool_search` tool. If `tool_search` is available, the apps that are searchable by `tools_search` will be listed by it.
Do not additionally call list_mcp_resources or list_mcp_resource_templates for apps.
</apps_instructions>

<plugins_instructions>
## Plugins
A plugin is a local bundle of skills, MCP servers, and apps.
### How to use plugins
- Skill naming: If a plugin contributes skills, those skill entries are prefixed with `plugin_name:` in the Skills list.
- MCP naming: Plugin-provided MCP tools keep standard MCP identifiers such as `mcp__server__tool`; use tool provenance to tell which plugin they come from.
- Trigger rules: If the user explicitly names a plugin, prefer capabilities associated with that plugin for that turn.
- Relationship to capabilities: Plugins are not invoked directly. Use their underlying skills, MCP tools, and app tools to help solve the task.
- Relevance: Determine what a plugin can help with from explicit user mention or from the plugin-associated skills, MCP tools, and apps exposed elsewhere in this turn.
- Missing/blocked: If the user requests a plugin that does not have relevant callable capabilities for the task, say so briefly and continue with the best fallback.
</plugins_instructions>

## developer

You are `/root`, the primary agent in a team of agents collaborating to fulfill the user's goals.

At the start of your turn, you are the active agent.
You can spawn sub-agents to handle subtasks, and those sub-agents can spawn their own sub-agents.
All agents in the team, including the agents that you can assign tasks to, are equally intelligent and capable, and have access to the same set of tools.

You can use `spawn_agent` to create a new agent, `followup_task` to give an existing agent a new task and trigger a turn, and `send_message` to pass a message to a running agent without triggering a turn.
Child agents can also spawn their own sub-agents.
You can decide how much context you want to propagate to your sub-agents with the `fork_turns` parameter.

You will receive messages in the analysis channel in the form:
```
Message Type: MESSAGE | FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: <recipient>
Sender: <author>
Payload:
<payload text>
```
They may be addressed as to=/root

Note that collaboration tools cannot be called from inside `functions.exec`. Call `spawn_agent`, `send_message`, `followup_task`, `wait_agent`, `interrupt_agent`, and `list_agents` only as direct tool calls using the recipient shown in their tool definitions, such as `to=functions.collaboration.spawn_agent`, since they are intentionally absent from the `functions.exec` `tools.*` namespace. Available tools in `functions.exec` are explicitly described with a `tools` namespace in the developer message.

All agents share the same directory. In detail:
- All agents have access to the same container and filesystem as you.
- All agents use the same current working directory.
- As a result, edits made by one agent are immediately visible to all other agents.

When calling `wait_agent`, prefer longer waits (minutes) to avoid busy polling.

There are 4 available concurrency slots, meaning that up to 4 agents can be active at once, including you.

Full-history forks (`fork_turns` omitted or `"all"`) inherit the parent model and reasoning effort and do not accept overrides. Only set `model` or `reasoning_effort` when explicitly requested by the user, applicable `AGENTS.md` instructions, or skill instructions; when doing so, set `fork_turns` to `"none"` or a positive integer string.

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: ~25 one-to-one sessions a week plus
consultations. Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

*Written 2026-09-07 from observed behavior. Correct it whenever it's wrong — that is the
point of the file.*

# Jay 2 — operating instructions for Codex (ai-os)

The canonical root is the repository directory containing this file (`C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` on this Windows machine). Resolve paths from the repository root instead of assuming a saved Mac or Windows absolute path. This is the router: it holds pointers, not knowledge. Read it, then follow the routing map to the right file instead of searching blind. Twin for Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`.

## Identity and mission

You are **Jay 2**, Joshua's executive assistant. This root is his EA workspace and command center. Joshua is the founder of Aspiring Attorneys and builder of LSAT U (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform; he is the product, voice, and content authority. Your job: take operations, admin, drafting, and research off his plate so his time goes to teaching and building. Frame work as decisions and tradeoffs, not basics.

Standing duties, run from here without re-briefing: student operations (transcripts, assignments, follow-ups via `students/`), prospect and consultation correspondence (drafted in his voice, **never auto-sent unless a pre-approved template**), platform actions on lsatprep.study, session-note ingestion from Gmail/Drive, and keeping the wiki ledgers (`tasks`/`hot`/`log`) current so the next session starts oriented.

## Routing map

Paths are relative to the repository root.

| Need | Path |
|---|---|
| **Who Joshua is** (identity, preferences, contact) | `context/about-me.md` |
| **The business** (offers, money, channels, gaps) | `context/work.md` |
| **Current priorities** (the 90-day slate) | `context/priorities.md` |
| **What we decided and why** (locked decisions) | `decisions/log.md` |
| Freshest session state (hot cache) | `wiki/hot.md` |
| Master index · work ledger · dated history | `wiki/index.md` · `wiki/tasks.md` · `wiki/log.md` |
| Product ground truth (pointers to live backends) | `wiki/dashboard.md` |
| LSAT teaching knowledge | `corpus/index.md` |
| A student — profile, transcripts, work, correspondence | `students/<name>/` — start at `students/README.md` |
| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |
| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |
| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |
| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |
| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |
| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |
| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |
| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |
| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |
| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` — one inbox, one archive |
| Archived web clips, with provenance + hashes | `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` — clip + `PROVENANCE.md` + `SHA256SUMS` |
| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |
| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |
| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |
| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |
| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/inbox/` — unprocessed only; `ingest` moves them to `raw/sources/clips/` when done |
| Application code | `projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |
| Other projects (life-path, …) | `projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |

## Retrieval order

1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.
2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.
3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.

**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.

**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua's voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.

**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.

## Voice

- **No em-dashes, ever.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust.
- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never "delve," "tapestry," "testament," "demystify," "boasts," "in the realm of," and their kin.
- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.
- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don't over-hedge.
- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.

## Tools, keys, and secrets

- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua's single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` directly in the editor.
- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.
- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.
- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.
- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.
- **No Git remote**, ever, without first completing the scrub checklist in `wiki/HANDLING.md`. The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.

## Skills

Everything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.

Active in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:

| Skill | Use it when |
|---|---|
| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua's explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |
| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |
| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |
| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |
| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |
| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |
| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |
| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |
| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |

When a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Runtime-specific catalog differences must be justified in `skills/PARITY.md`; undeclared differences are defects.

## Guardrails

- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.
- **Root stays thin.** Don't accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.
- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.
- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.
- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don't grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.
- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.
- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.
- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.
- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU's recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua's explicit approval.
- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.
- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.

## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)

- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.
- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.
- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.
- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex runs the catalog minus the wiki/Obsidian and `graphify` skills, so the two skill tables legitimately differ.
- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.

Use the absolute paths above.\r\n\r\n## Project Map\r\n\r\n- `src/App.tsx`: route registration and top-level app wiring.\r\n- `src/pages/`: route-level screens, including the Foyer and admin/teacher surfaces.\r\n- `src/components/`: shared UI, including `foyer/`, `inbox/`, and shadcn/ui primitives.\r\n- `src/hooks/`: auth, permissions, and shared data hooks.\r\n- `src/lib/`: drill, WAJ, gamification, and other product logic.\r\n- `src/integrations/supabase/`: generated Supabase client and types. Do not hand-edit generated types.\r\n- `supabase/functions/`: Edge Functions.\r\n- `supabase/migrations/`: database schema migrations.\r\n\r\n## Stack And Commands\r\n\r\n- Stack: React 18, Vite 5, TypeScript, Tailwind, shadcn/ui, Supabase, TanStack Query.\r\n- Package manager: `npm`. `package-lock.json` is canonical. Ignore stray Bun lockfiles.\r\n- Dev server: `npm run dev` on port `8080`.\r\n- Build: `npm run build`\r\n- Lint: `npm run lint`\r\n- Test: `npm run test`\r\n- Watch tests: `npm run test:watch`\r\n- Typecheck: `npm run typecheck`. Required before a push — `vite build` strips types without checking them, so a type error would ship. `.claude/safe-push.sh` gates on it.\r\n- E2E: `npm run test:e2e` (Playwright, `e2e/smoke.spec.ts`).\r\n\r\n## Working Rules\r\n\r\n- Keep changes small and aligned with existing patterns.\r\n- Use TypeScript React function components and the `@/` path alias.\r\n- Preserve Lovable-specific plumbing, including the `lovable-tagger` Vite plugin, the PWA setup, and the service-worker guard in `src/main.tsx`.\r\n- Do not change app behavior just to satisfy tooling setup.\r\n- Do not hardcode secrets or service-role credentials.\r\n- Treat Supabase-generated files as generated artifacts, not hand-edited source.\r\n- TypeScript strictness is intentionally loose. Do not tighten it without explicit approval.\r\n- Keep this adapter aligned with `CLAUDE.md` without duplicating the full project guide. Put durable detail in `CLAUDE.md`; keep shared reference material in `docs/`, `references/`, or `templates/`.\r\n\r\n## Product Constraints\r\n\r\n- The Foyer is the primary navigation surface. The `GooeyDock` quick-launch row belongs only on `/foyer`.\r\n- Inbox is a real 1:1 student-admin messaging system with PDF attachments.\r\n- Admin surfaces must stay responsive on desktop and mobile.\r\n- Prefer truthful fallbacks over fake buttons, fake analytics, or placeholder behavior.\r\n- Onboarding asks for name, not username. Sign-in uses email. The instructor display name is `Joshua`.\r\n\r\n## Smart Drill Note\r\n\r\nOn `/drill`, adaptive Smart Drill can show a `currentQuestion` that is not the same as `session.questionQueue[session.currentIndex]`. Any per-question UI state must key off `currentQuestion.qid` unless the code path is explicitly limited to queued drills.\r\n\r\n## Verification (must not skip)\r\n\r\n- Any change to `src/components/` or `src/pages/` is not "done" until it has been viewed running in the app (dev server + a browser tool), with a screenshot or DOM check as evidence. Never report a UI change complete on the strength of the diff alone. This is the single biggest recurring failure across past sessions — a hard gate, not a suggestion.\r\n- **Responsive fixes carry a wider gate: verify across the full emulate matrix BEFORE pushing, not after Joshua reports it.** Narrow, mid, and wide viewports plus the sidecar-docked case, every time. A pushed "fix" that does not hold costs a second full session; this has already happened once.\r\n\r\n## Workflow Expectations\r\n\r\n- Run `npm run build` before wrapping up significant code or documentation work.\r\n- If tests are relevant to the change, run them too.\r\n- After completing a change, stage the intended files, commit with a concise message, and push to `origin main` unless the user says otherwise.\r\n- **Push via `.claude/safe-push.sh`, not raw `git push`.** Lovable auto-commits to `main` mid-session, so a raw push can be rejected or race a concurrent commit. The script fetches, merges `origin/main` if it diverged (never rebases, never force-pushes), then pushes. It also gates on `npm run typecheck`.\r\n- Existing Codex repo hooks live in `.codex/hooks.json`; preserve them unless the task explicitly requires a hook change.\r\n\r\n## Session Protocol (second brain)\r\n\r\nProduct context, status, and decisions live in the Obsidian vault at `~/ai-os/`, not this repo. Two repo-local skills in `.agents/skills/` run the session ritual; the canonical protocol is in the vault `CLAUDE.md`.\r\n\r\nThe vault was rebuilt 2026-07-16 (paths relative to `~/ai-os`). Live: teaching voice `wiki/teaching-voice.md` (route ALL student-facing copy through it), the business `wiki/entities/aspiring-attorneys.md`, a student\'s record `students/<name>/` (see `students/README.md`), teaching knowledge `corpus/index.md`, and the `wiki/{tasks,hot,dashboard,index,log}.md` hubs named below. Archived read-only reference (pre-rebuild product knowledge, may be stale): `archive/wiki-pre-rebuild-2026-07-16/domains/lsat-u/` — product context `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-product.md`, design system `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-design.md`, quality bar `…/synthesis/bootcamp-quality-bar.md`, domain index `…/index.md`.\r\n\r\n- `pickup` — start of session. Read `wiki/tasks.md` (the Now / Next / Done ledger) first, then `wiki/hot.md`, `wiki/dashboard.md`, skim `wiki/index.md`, read the latest `wiki/log.md` entry. Emit a 6-8 line briefing (where we left off, Now, Next, open threads, hot, suggested next) and wait. Do not deep-read other pages until pointed at a task.\r\n- `wrapup` — end of session. Propose which `## Now` tasks finished and confirm before writing; a wrong "Done" is the failure to avoid. Then strike finished items through in `wiki/tasks.md` with a date, refresh `wiki/hot.md`, append a dated `wiki/log.md` entry (newest first, distinct label for same-day sessions), update the task anchor.\r\n\r\n`wiki/tasks.md` is the single source of truth for what is open vs done. Finished work is struck through so a new session never re-runs it. `raw/` in the vault is immutable.\r\n\r\n## Codex Adapter Layout\r\n\r\n- `.codex/config.toml`: minimal shared Codex project config. Keep secrets and machine-specific overrides out of this file.\r\n- `.codex/agents/`: Codex agent definitions converted from `.claude/agents/*.md` when Claude agents exist. Use `.toml` files with converted instructions in `developer_instructions`.\r\n- `.agents/skills/`: repository-local Codex skills copied from `.claude/skills/` when Claude skills exist and Codex should use them. Keep each `SKILL.md` with its supporting files.\r\n- `.codex/hooks.json`: existing repository hooks. Preserve unless the task explicitly concerns hooks.\r\n\r\nThis checkout currently has Claude settings and hooks under `.claude/`, but no `.claude/skills/` or `.claude/agents/` directory to convert.\r\n\r\n## Known Gaps\r\n\r\n- Vitest specs exist (80 `src/**/*.test.ts(x)` files as of 2026-07-16); run with `npm run test`. Playwright is wired with one e2e spec (`e2e/smoke.spec.ts`), run via `npm run test:e2e`.\r\n- No Prettier config and no CI workflows.\r\n- `README.md` is still minimal.\r\n\r\n## Agent skills\r\n\r\n### Issue tracker\r\n\r\nGitHub Issues on `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db`, via the `gh` CLI. See `docs/agents/domain.md`.\r\n\r\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '# LSAT Prep Scheduling Control Center Implementation Plan\n\n> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.\n\n**Goal:** Ship one local, cross-platform `lsat-schedule` CLI and local MCP that give Joshua complete LSAT Prep scheduling control without changing Cal.com.\n\n**Architecture:** A new authenticated `scheduling-admin` Edge Function owns every privileged live mutation and returns typed snapshots. A small TypeScript client, domain types, preview builder, and command schemas are shared by the interactive CLI, direct CLI commands, and the local stdio MCP. PostgreSQL remains the scheduling source of truth and receives append-only audit rows plus undoable configuration state.\n\n**Tech Stack:** TypeScript, Node.js 22, `vite-node`, `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk`, Supabase Edge Functions (Deno), PostgreSQL migrations, Vitest, Deno tests.\n\n## Global Constraints\n\n- LSAT Prep only. Do not call, read, migrate, or modify Cal.com.\n- The public booking page stays separate; visitors see their local timezone and operator commands use `America/Toronto`.\n- Use the existing Google Calendar connection and existing Stripe hold, expiry, and refund paths.\n- Existing booked sessions retain their original terms. Configuration changes affect future bookings only.\n- Every CLI mutation shows exact before/after values and asks `Continue? y/N`; default is no change.\n- MCP writes require preview, then a short-lived confirmation token tied to that exact preview.\n- Deactivate links and passes; do not expose permanent deletion.\n- Cancellation/reschedule messages default to no message. Create a draft only when requested; never send it automatically.\n- Store no credentials in source, migrations, audit data, test fixtures, or command output.\n\n---\n\n## Locked file structure\n\n| Path | Responsibility |\n|---|---|\n| `supabase/migrations/<timestamp>_scheduling_control_center.sql` | Audit schema, date overrides, event schedule references, scoped RPCs, and undo transaction. |\n| `supabase/functions/_shared/schedulingAdmin.ts` | Input validation, DTOs, preview token hashing, and action dispatch helpers that do not depend on HTTP. |\n| `supabase/functions/scheduling-admin/index.ts` | Admin JWT gate and HTTP adapter for overview, previews, applies, and read-only scheduling actions. |\n| `supabase/functions/scheduling-admin/index_test.ts` | Deno tests for admin authorization and action boundaries. |\n| `scripts/scheduling/types.ts` | Shared Node-side command/action DTOs. |\n| `scripts/scheduling/client.ts` | Authenticated calls to `scheduling-admin`; no terminal or MCP code. |\n| `scripts/scheduling/preview.ts` | Human-readable before/after formatter and confirmation-token handling. |\n| `scripts/scheduling/interactive.ts` | Readline menu and guided link, booking-pass, availability, booking, and undo flows. |\n| `scripts/lsat-schedule.ts` | Direct-command parser and interactive CLI entry point. |\n| `scripts/lsat-scheduling-mcp.ts` | Local stdio MCP server that maps tools to the shared client. |\n| `bin/lsat-schedule.cjs` | Cross-platform `npm link` launcher for the installable command. |\n| `scripts/scheduling/*.test.ts` | Vitest unit tests for each local boundary. |\n| `package.json` | `schedule`, `schedule:mcp`, and `bin` entries plus only required runtime packages. |\n\n### Task 1: Add the scheduling-control database contract\n\n**Files:**\n- Create: `supabase/migrations/<timestamp>_scheduling_control_center.sql`\n- Modify: `supabase/tests/booking/minimal_schema.sql`\n- Modify: `supabase/tests/booking/single_session_lifecycle.sql`\n\n**Interfaces:**\n- Produces `public.scheduling_audit_log`, `public.booking_date_overrides`, `public.booking_schedules`.\n- Produces `public.scheduling_preview(_action jsonb)`, `public.apply_scheduling_action(_action jsonb, _preview_token uuid, _actor_id uuid, _interface text, _device_name text)`, and `public.undo_scheduling_audit(_audit_id uuid, _actor_id uuid, _interface text, _device_name text)`.\n- `scheduling_preview` returns `{ token, expires_at, action, before, after, summary }` and stores a SHA-256 hash of the canonical action plus before snapshot. `apply_scheduling_action` rejects an expired or mismatched token.\n\n- [ ] **Step 1: Write failing SQL assertions for immutable history and confirmation binding**\n\nAdd to `single_session_lifecycle.sql`:\n\n```sql\nSELECT public.assert_raises(\n  $$UPDATE public.scheduling_audit_log SET action = \'changed\'$$,\n  \'audit_log_is_append_only\'\n);\nSELECT public.assert_raises(\n  $$SELECT public.apply_scheduling_action(\'{"kind":"event.update","slug":"single-session"}\'::jsonb,\n      \'00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000\'::uuid,\n      \'00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000001\'::uuid, \'cli\', \'test\')$$,\n  \'preview_not_found\'\n);\n```\n\n- [ ] **Step 2: Run the database test and verify it fails because the contract does not exist**\n\nRun: `npm run test:booking:db`  \nExpected: FAIL mentioning `scheduling_audit_log` or `apply_scheduling_action`.\n\n- [ ] **Step 3: Create the minimal schema and action allowlist**\n\nImplement an append-only audit table with `id`, `created_at`, `actor_id`, `interface`, `device_name`, `kind`, `target`, `before_state`, `after_state`, `undo_of`, and `undone_by`. Add a separate short-lived preview table with `token`, `action`, `before_state`, `expires_at`, and `used_at`. Add row-level triggers that raise `audit_log_is_append_only` on update/delete.\n\nAdd schedule and override data only where the slot functions consume it: `booking_schedules(id, name, timezone)`, `booking_availability.schedule_id`, `booking_event_types.schedule_id`, and `booking_date_overrides(schedule_id, local_date, closed, ranges)`. Seed the current weekly availability as the default schedule and backfill all existing events to it. Modify slot functions so an override wins over weekly hours, but always retain Google Calendar conflict checks in the claim path.\n\n`apply_scheduling_action` must only accept these `kind` values: `event.create`, `event.update`, `event.deactivate`, `availability.replace`, `override.upsert`, `override.deactivate`, `pass.create`, `pass.update`, `pass.revoke`, `booking.cancel`, `booking.reschedule`, and `booking.refund`. Reject any other kind with `invalid_scheduling_action`.\n\n- [ ] **Step 4: Run the database test and verify it passes**\n\nRun: `npm run test:booking:db`  \nExpected: PASS, including existing booking lifecycle and conflict assertions.\n\n- [ ] **Step 5: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add supabase/migrations supabase/tests/booking\ngit commit -m "feat: add scheduling control database contract"\n```\n\n### Task 2: Add one secure admin scheduling API\n\n**Files:**\n- Create: `supabase/functions/_shared/schedulingAdmin.ts`\n- Create: `supabase/functions/scheduling-admin/index.ts`\n- Create: `supabase/functions/scheduling-admin/index_test.ts`\n\n**Interfaces:**\n- Consumes the Task 1 RPCs and the existing admin JWT / `user_roles` pattern in `supabase/functions/booking-pass-admin/index.ts`.\n- Produces HTTP actions `overview`, `read`, `preview`, `apply`, `undo`, and `draft_message`.\n- `overview` returns `{ timezone, events, links, passes, upcoming_bookings, calendar_health }`.\n\n- [ ] **Step 1: Write failing Deno tests for authorization and safe write flow**\n\n```ts\nDeno.test("apply rejects a write without an admin claim", async () => {\n  const response = await invoke({ action: "apply", token: "not-admin" });\n  assertEquals(response.status, 403);\n});\n\nDeno.test("apply requires the preview token returned by preview", async () => {\n  const response = await invoke({ action: "apply", token: adminJwt, preview_token: crypto.randomUUID() });\n  assertEquals(response.status, 409);\n});\n```\n\n- [ ] **Step 2: Run the tests and verify they fail**\n\nRun: `deno test --allow-env --allow-net supabase/functions/scheduling-admin/index_test.ts`  \nExpected: FAIL because the module does not exist.\n\n- [ ] **Step 3: Implement the Edge Function as a thin HTTP adapter**\n\nReuse the JWT claim validation and `user_roles.role = \'admin\'` check from `booking-pass-admin`. Do not accept service-role keys from clients. Route each action to `schedulingAdmin.ts`; use service role only after the caller is verified as admin.\n\nFor `booking.cancel`, `booking.reschedule`, and `booking.refund`, call the existing authoritative booking functions/RPCs rather than copying Stripe or Google Calendar logic. Make refund a separate action. Return draft-message data only, never call the transactional email sender.\n\n- [ ] **Step 4: Run the Deno tests and existing booking edge tests**\n\nRun: `deno test --allow-env --allow-net supabase/functions/scheduling-admin/index_test.ts`  \nExpected: PASS.\n\nRun: `npm run test:booking:edge`  \nExpected: PASS.\n\n- [ ] **Step 5: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add supabase/functions/_shared/schedulingAdmin.ts supabase/functions/scheduling-admin\ngit commit -m "feat: add scheduling admin API"\n```\n\n### Task 3: Build the shared local client and preview discipline\n\n**Files:**\n- Create: `scripts/scheduling/types.ts`\n- Create: `scripts/scheduling/client.ts`\n- Create: `scripts/scheduling/preview.ts`\n- Create: `scripts/scheduling/client.test.ts`\n- Create: `scripts/scheduling/preview.test.ts`\n\n**Interfaces:**\n- `SchedulingClient.overview(): Promise<SchedulingOverview>`\n- `SchedulingClient.preview(action: SchedulingAction): Promise<SchedulingPreview>`\n- `SchedulingClient.apply(previewToken: string): Promise<SchedulingMutationResult>`\n- `SchedulingClient.undo(auditId: string): Promise<SchedulingMutationResult>`\n- `formatPreview(preview): string` includes environment, target, exact before/after values, and summary.\n\n- [ ] **Step 1: Write failing Vitest cases**\n\n```ts\nit("does not call apply when confirmation is not y", async () => {\n  const client = fakeClient();\n  await confirmAndApply(client, preview, async () => "n");\n  expect(client.apply).not.toHaveBeenCalled();\n});\n\nit("renders Toronto time for an operator slot", () => {\n  expect(formatToronto("2026-09-15T17:00:00.000Z")).toContain("1:00 PM");\n});\n```\n\n- [ ] **Step 2: Run the tests and verify they fail**\n\nRun: `npx vitest run scripts/scheduling/client.test.ts scripts/scheduling/preview.test.ts`  \nExpected: FAIL because the modules do not exist.\n\n- [ ] **Step 3: Implement the shared client**\n\nRead credentials from the same ignored local `.env` convention as `scripts/booking-link.ts`. Sign in as the LSAT Prep admin, invoke `scheduling-admin`, normalize failures into typed errors, and never print credentials. Put every Node-side scheduling DTO in `types.ts`; neither CLI nor MCP may construct raw HTTP payloads.\n\n- [ ] **Step 4: Run the tests**\n\nRun: `npx vitest run scripts/scheduling/client.test.ts scripts/scheduling/preview.test.ts`  \nExpected: PASS.\n\n- [ ] **Step 5: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add scripts/scheduling\ngit commit -m "feat: add shared scheduling client"\n```\n\n### Task 4: Ship the installable, guided CLI\n\n**Files:**\n- Create: `scripts/scheduling/interactive.ts`\n- Create: `scripts/lsat-schedule.ts`\n- Create: `bin/lsat-schedule.cjs`\n- Create: `scripts/scheduling/interactive.test.ts`\n- Modify: `package.json`\n\n**Interfaces:**\n- Direct groups: `links`, `events`, `availability`, `overrides`, `passes`, `bookings`, `payments`, `history`, and `calendar`.\n- `lsat-schedule` with no arguments calls `overview()` and opens the numbered menu.\n- `--json` returns machine-readable data for a read or completed mutation, with no menu decoration.\n\n- [ ] **Step 1: Write failing CLI tests**\n\n```ts\nit("starts with a useful overview", async () => {\n  const output = await runCli([], fakeClientWithOverview());\n  expect(output).toContain("Active links");\n  expect(output).toContain("Upcoming bookings");\n});\n\nit("creates a paid reusable link only after y confirmation", async () => {\n  const output = await runCli(["links", "create", "--reusable", "--paid", "--price", "45"], fakeClient(), ["y"]);\n  expect(output).toContain("Continue? y/N");\n});\n```\n\n- [ ] **Step 2: Run the tests and verify they fail**\n\nRun: `npx vitest run scripts/scheduling/interactive.test.ts`  \nExpected: FAIL because the CLI entry point does not exist.\n\n- [ ] **Step 3: Implement direct commands and the guided menu**\n\nThe link wizard must ask link type (reusable, single-use, pinned), payment state (paid or free), then only relevant fields. A standard link edit changes future bookings only. The overview lists all active links, their purpose, price, duration, payment state, and URL before the menu.\n\nImplement `undo <audit-id>`, `history list`, and `calendar status`. For cancellation or rescheduling, prompt `Create a notification draft? y/N`; only call `draft_message` after `y`.\n\nAdd `"schedule": "vite-node scripts/lsat-schedule.ts --"`, `"schedule:mcp": "vite-node scripts/lsat-scheduling-mcp.ts"`, and a `bin` entry. The CJS launcher must spawn Node with the package-local TypeScript runner so `npm link` exposes `lsat-schedule` on macOS and Windows. Document `npm link` and local `.env` setup in `--help`; do not create a separate installer.\n\n- [ ] **Step 4: Run CLI tests and a non-mutating smoke test**\n\nRun: `npx vitest run scripts/scheduling/interactive.test.ts`  \nExpected: PASS.\n\nRun: `npm run schedule -- --help`  \nExpected: grouped help including `links`, `passes`, `bookings`, `history`, and `calendar`.\n\n- [ ] **Step 5: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add scripts/scheduling scripts/lsat-schedule.ts bin/lsat-schedule.cjs package.json package-lock.json\ngit commit -m "feat: add lsat scheduling CLI"\n```\n\n### Task 5: Add the local MCP adapter and end-to-end verification\n\n**Files:**\n- Create: `scripts/lsat-scheduling-mcp.ts`\n- Create: `scripts/scheduling/mcp.test.ts`\n- Modify: `package.json`\n- Modify: `docs/ops/native-single-session-workers.md` only if deployment adds a new Edge Function release step.\n\n**Interfaces:**\n- MCP read tools: `scheduling_overview`, `scheduling_read`, `scheduling_calendar_status`, `scheduling_history`.\n- MCP write tools: `scheduling_preview`, `scheduling_apply`, `scheduling_undo`, `scheduling_draft_message`.\n- `scheduling_apply` accepts only a token emitted by `scheduling_preview`; it never accepts a raw scheduling action.\n\n- [ ] **Step 1: Write failing MCP tests**\n\n```ts\nit("exposes preview and apply as separate tools", async () => {\n  const tools = await listMcpTools(makeServer(fakeClient()));\n  expect(tools).toContain("scheduling_preview");\n  expect(tools).toContain("scheduling_apply");\n});\n\nit("does not expose a raw mutation tool", async () => {\n  const tools = await listMcpTools(makeServer(fakeClient()));\n  expect(tools).not.toContain("scheduling_mutate");\n});\n```\n\n- [ ] **Step 2: Run the tests and verify they fail**\n\nRun: `npx vitest run scripts/scheduling/mcp.test.ts`  \nExpected: FAIL because the MCP server does not exist.\n\n- [ ] **Step 3: Implement a stdio-only MCP server**\n\nAdd `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` as the only new runtime dependency. Build the server from the Task 3 client. Describe write tools in plain language, return preview data to the caller, and require the returned token for apply. Do not add HTTP listening, remote credentials, Cal.com tools, or an automatic-email tool.\n\n- [ ] **Step 4: Run the full verification set**\n\nRun: `npx vitest run scripts/scheduling`  \nExpected: PASS.\n\nRun: `npm run test:booking`  \nExpected: PASS.\n\nRun: `npm run typecheck`  \nExpected: PASS.\n\nRun: `npm run build`  \nExpected: PASS.\n\nPerform one read-only production smoke test: `npm run schedule -- overview`. Verify that the active link list and prices match `booking_event_types`; do not run an apply command.\n\n- [ ] **Step 5: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add scripts/lsat-scheduling-mcp.ts scripts/scheduling/mcp.test.ts package.json package-lock.json docs/ops\ngit commit -m "feat: add local scheduling MCP"\n```\n\n## Plan self-review\n\n- **Spec coverage:** Tasks 1-5 cover all approved areas: event links and prices, free/paid and reusable/single-use/pinned link modes, passes, bookings, refunds, recurring availability, date overrides, Google Calendar safety, preview/confirmation, draft-only messages, audit/undo, CLI overview/menu/direct commands/JSON, local MCP, Toronto operator time, and visitor-local display.\n- **Deliberate exclusions:** Cal.com, a hosted MCP, calendar OAuth or calendar selection, permanent deletion, automatic sending, reversal of payments or sent messages, and the later website admin surface are not planned.\n- **Consistency:** The only mutation path is preview then apply. CLI confirmation wraps the same token used by the MCP. The shared host-day advisory\n# lock plus the occupying exclusion constraint must let exactly one commit.\nrace_start="$("${psql[@]}" -Atc "SELECT race_start FROM public.single_session_test_runtime")"\nrace_sql_a="SELECT id FROM public.claim_single_session_slot(\'$race_start\'::timestamptz,\'Race A\',\'race-a@example.com\',NULL,\'[]\'::jsonb,\'idem-real-race-a\',repeat(\'1\',32),repeat(\'2\',32),NULL);"\nrace_sql_b="SELECT id FROM public.claim_single_session_slot(\'$race_start\'::timestamptz,\'Race B\',\'race-b@example.com\',NULL,\'[]\'::jsonb,\'idem-real-race-b\',repeat(\'3\',32),repeat(\'4\',32),NULL);"\nset +e\n"${psql[@]}" -c "$race_sql_a" >/dev/null 2>&1 &\nrace_pid_a=$!\n"${psql[@]}" -c "$race_sql_b" >/dev/null 2>&1 &\nrace_pid_b=$!\nwait "$race_pid_a"\nrace_status_a=$?\nwait "$race_pid_b"\nrace_status_b=$?\nset -e\nif { test "$race_status_a" -eq 0 && test "$race_status_b" -eq 0; } || \\\n   { test "$race_status_a" -ne 0 && test "$race_status_b" -ne 0; }; then\n  echo "native claim race did not produce exactly one winner" >&2\n  exit 1\nfi\nrace_winners="$("${psql[@]}" -Atc "SELECT count(*) FROM public.single_session_bookings WHERE client_idempotency_key IN (\'idem-real-race-a\',\'idem-real-race-b\')")"\ntest "$race_winners" = "1"\n\n# Cross-engine race: a package redemption and a native Hold contend for the\n# same clean slot. Both paths must use the same host-day advisory lock.\ncross_race_start="$("${psql[@]}" -Atc "SELECT cross_race_start FROM public.single_session_test_runtime")"\ncross_race_pass="$("${psql[@]}" -Atc "SELECT cross_race_pass_id FROM public.single_session_test_runtime")"\ncross_native_sql="SELECT id FROM public.claim_single_session_slot(\'$cross_race_start\'::timestamptz,\'Cross Native\',\'cross-native@example.com\',NULL,\'[]\'::jsonb,\'idem-real-cross-native\',repeat(\'5\',32),repeat(\'6\',32),NULL);"\ncross_package_sql="SELECT redemption_id FROM public.redeem_booking_pass_v2(\'$cross_race_pass\'::uuid,\'idem-real-cross-package\',\'$cross_race_start\'::timestamptz,NULL,\'Cross Package\',\'cross-package@example.com\');"\nset +e\n"${psql[@]}" -c "$cross_native_sql" >/dev/null 2>&1 &\ncross_pid_native=$!\n"${psql[@]}" -c "$cross_package_sql" >/dev/null 2>&1 &\ncross_pid_package=$!\nwait "$cross_pid_native"\ncross_status_native=$?\nwait "$cross_pid_package"\ncross_status_package=$?\nset -e\nif { test "$cross_status_native" -eq 0 && test "$cross_status_package" -eq 0; } || \\\n   { test "$cross_status_native" -ne 0 && test "$cross_status_package" -ne 0; }; then\n  echo "cross-engine claim race did not produce exactly one winner" >&2\n  exit 1\nfi\ncross_native_count="$("${psql[@]}" -Atc "SELECT count(*) FROM public.single_session_bookings WHERE client_idempotency_key=\'idem-real-cross-native\'")"\ncross_package_count="$("${psql[@]}" -Atc "SELECT count(*) FROM public.booking_pass_redemptions WHERE booking_pass_id=\'$cross_race_pass\' AND idempotency_key=\'idem-real-cross-package\'")"\ncross_used="$("${psql[@]}" -Atc "SELECT used_credits FROM public.booking_passes WHERE id=\'$cross_race_pass\'")"\ntest $((cross_native_count + cross_package_count)) -eq 1\ntest "$cross_used" = "$cross_package_count"\n\n"${psql[@]}" -f "$repo_dir/supabase/tests/booking/phase_concurrency.sql" >/dev/null\n\ncancel_sql="SELECT public.cancel_booking_redemption(\'20000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000001\', true, NULL);"\n"${psql[@]}" -c "$cancel_sql" >/dev/null &\nfirst_pid=$!\n"${psql[@]}" -c "$cancel_sql" >/dev/null &\nsecond_pid=$!\nwait "$first_pid"\nwait "$second_pid"\n\nresult="$("${psql[@]}" -Atc "SELECT used_credits || \':\' || status FROM public.booking_passes WHERE id=\'10000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000001\'")"\nrefund="$("${psql[@]}" -Atc "SELECT credit_refunded::text || \':\' || status || \':\' || calendar_action FROM public.booking_pass_redemptions WHERE id=\'20000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000001\'")"\ntest "$result" = "0:active"\ntest "$refund" = "true:cancelled:delete"\n\necho "booking database migrations and double-cancel concurrency: PASS"\n\r\n'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 3.3 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': "-- Privileged scheduling configuration, preview binding, and append-only history.\n\nCREATE TABLE public.booking_schedules (\n  id uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),\n  name text NOT NULL,\n  timezone text NOT NULL,\n  created_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),\n  updated_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now()\n);\n\nINSERT INTO public.booking_schedules (name, timezone)\nSELECT 'Default', COALESCE((SELECT timezone FROM public.booking_availability_config WHERE id = 1), 'America/Toronto')\nWHERE NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM public.booking_schedules);\n\nALTER TABLE public.booking_availability\n  ADD COLUMN schedule_id uuid REFERENCES public.booking_schedules(id);\nUPDATE public.booking_availability\nSET schedule_id = (SELECT id FROM public.booking_schedules ORDER BY created_at, id LIMIT 1)\nWHERE schedule_id IS NULL;\nALTER TABLE public.booking_availability ALTER COLUMN schedule_id SET NOT NULL;\n\nALTER TABLE public.booking_event_types\n  ADD COLUMN schedule_id uuid REFERENCES public.booking_schedules(id);\nUPDATE public.booking_event_types\nSET schedule_id = (SELECT id FROM public.booking_schedules ORDER BY created_at, id LIMIT 1)\nWHERE schedule_id IS NULL;\nALTER TABLE public.booking_event_types ALTER COLUMN schedule_id SET NOT NULL;\n\nCREATE TABLE public.booking_date_overrides (\n  schedule_id uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES public.booking_schedules(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,\n  local_date date NOT NULL,\n  closed boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false,\n  ranges jsonb NOT NULL DEFAULT '[]'::jsonb CHECK (jsonb_typeof(ranges) = 'array'),\n  active boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT true,\n  created_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),\n  updated_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),\n  PRIMARY KEY (schedule_id, local_date)\n);\n\nCREATE TABLE public.scheduling_audit_log (\n  id uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),\n  created_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),\n  actor_id uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES auth.users(id),\n  interface text NOT NULL CHECK (interface IN ('cli', 'mcp')),\n  device_name text NOT NULL,\n  kind text NOT NULL,\n  target jsonb NOT NULL,\n  before_state jsonb NOT NULL,\n  after_state jsonb NOT NULL,\n  undo_of uuid REFERENCES public.scheduling_audit_log(id)\n);\nCREATE UNIQUE INDEX scheduling_audit_one_undo\n  ON public.scheduling_audit_log (undo_of) WHERE undo_of IS NOT NULL;\n\nCREATE FUNCTION public.reject_scheduling_audit_mutation()\nRETURNS trigger LANGUAGE plpgsql SET search_path = public AS $$\nBEGIN\n  RAISE EXCEPTION 'audit_log_is_append_only';\nEND;\n$$;\nCREATE TRIGGER scheduling_audit_no_update\n  BEFORE UPDATE ON public.scheduling_audit_log\n  FOR EACH ROW EXECUTE FUNCTION public.reject_scheduling_audit_mutation();\nCREATE TRIGGER scheduling_audit_no_delete\n  BEFORE DELETE ON public.scheduling_audit_log\n  FOR EACH ROW EXECUTE FUNCTION public.reject_scheduling_audit_mutation();\n\nCREATE TABLE public.scheduling_previews (\n  token uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),\n  action jsonb NOT NULL,\n  before_state jsonb NOT NULL,\n  action_hash bytea NOT NULL,\n  expires_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now() + interval '5 minutes',\n  used_at timestamptz\n);\n\nCREATE FUNCTION public.scheduling_action_is_allowed(_kind text)\nRETURNS boolean LANGUAGE sql IMMUTABLE AS $$\n  SELECT _kind = ANY (ARRAY[\n    'event.create', 'event.update', 'event.deactivate',\n    'availability.replace', 'override.upsert', 'override.deactivate',\n    'pass.create', 'pass.update', 'pass.revoke',\n    'booking.cancel', 'booking.reschedule', 'booking.refund'\n  ]);\n$$;\n\nCREATE FUNCTION public.scheduling_action_before(_action jsonb)\nRETURNS jsonb LANGUAGE plpgsql STABLE SET search_path = public AS $$\nDECLARE\n  _kind text := _action->>'kind';\n  _schedule_id uuid;\n  _result jsonb;\nBEGIN\n  IF NOT public.scheduling_action_is_allowed(_kind) THEN\n    RAISE EXCEPTION 'invalid_scheduling_action';\n  END IF;\n\n  CASE split_part(_kind, '.', 1)\n    WHEN 'event' THEN\n      SELECT to_jsonb(t) INTO _result FROM public.booking_event_types t WHERE t.slug = _action->>'slug';\n    WHEN 'availability' THEN\n      _schedule_id := (_action->>'schedule_id')::uuid;\n      SELECT COALESCE(jsonb_agg(to_jsonb(a) - 'id' - 'created_at' ORDER BY a.day_of_week, a.start_time), '[]'::jsonb)\n      INTO _result FROM public.booking_availability a WHERE a.schedule_id = _schedule_id;\n    WHEN 'override' THEN\n      SELECT to_jsonb(o) INTO _result FROM public.booking_date_overrides o\n      WHERE o.schedule_id = (_action->>'schedule_id')::uuid AND o.local_date = (_action->>'local_date')::date;\n    WHEN 'pass' THEN\n      SELECT to_jsonb(p) - 'token_hash' INTO _result\n      FROM public.booking_passes p WHERE p.id = (_action->>'id')::uuid;\n    WHEN 'booking' THEN\n      SELECT to_jsonb(r) INTO _result\n      FROM public.booking_pass_redemptions r WHERE r.id = (_action->>'id')::uuid;\n  END CASE;\n  RETURN COALESCE(_result, 'null'::jsonb);\nEND;\n$$;\n\nCREATE FUNCTION public.scheduling_preview(_action jsonb)\nRETURNS jsonb LANGUAGE plpgsql SECURITY DEFINER SET search_path = public AS $$\nDECLARE\n  _before jsonb;\n  _preview public.scheduling_previews%ROWTYPE;\n  _after jsonb;\nBEGIN\n  _before := public.scheduling_action_before(_action);\n  _after := COALESCE(_action->'values', _action - 'kind');\n  INSERT INTO public.scheduling_previews (action, before_state, action_hash)\n  VALUES (_action, _before, digest(_action::text || _before::text, 'sha256'))\n  RETURNING * INTO _preview;\n  RETURN jsonb_build_object(\n    'token', _preview.token, 'expires_at', _preview.expires_at, 'action', _action,\n    'before', _before, 'after', _after,\n    'summary', format('%s %s', _action->>'kind', COALESCE(_action->>'slug', _action->>'id', _action->>'local_date', 'schedule'))\n  );\nEND;\n$$;\n\nCREATE FUNCTION public.apply_scheduling_action(\n  _action jsonb, _preview_token uuid, _actor_id uuid, _interface text, _device_name text\n) RETURNS jsonb LANGUAGE plpgsql SECURITY DEFINER SET search_path = public AS $$\nDECLARE\n  _preview public.scheduling_previews%ROWTYPE;\n  _kind text := _action->>'kind';\n  _values jsonb := COALESCE(_action->'values', '{}'::jsonb);\n  _before jsonb;\n  _after jsonb;\n  _audit_id uuid;\n  _schedule_id uuid;\nBEGIN\n  IF NOT public.scheduling_action_is_allowed(_kind) THEN RAISE EXCEPTION 'invalid_scheduling_action'; END IF;\n  SELECT * INTO _preview FROM public.scheduling_previews WHERE token = _preview_token FOR UPDATE;\n  IF NOT FOUND THEN RAISE EXCEPTION 'preview_not_found'; END IF;\n  IF _preview.used_at IS NOT NULL THEN RAISE EXCEPTION 'preview_already_used'; END IF;\n  IF _preview.expires_at <= now() THEN RAISE EXCEPTION 'preview_expired'; END IF;\n  IF _preview.action <> _action\n     OR _preview.action_hash <> digest(_action::text || _preview.before_state::text, 'sha256')\n     OR public.scheduling_action_before(_action) <> _preview.before_state THEN\n    RAISE EXCEPTION 'preview_mismatch';\n  END IF;\n  _before := _preview.before_state;\n\n  CASE _kind\n    WHEN 'event.create' THEN\n      INSERT INTO public.booking_event_types (\n        slug, label, length_minutes, slot_interval_minutes, minimum_notice_minutes,\n        buffer_before_minutes, buffer_after_minutes, window_business_days,\n        price_cents, currency, requires_payment, active, schedule_id\n      ) VALUES (\n        _action->>'slug', _values->>'label', (_values->>'length_minutes')::int,\n        (_values->>'slot_interval_minutes')::int, COALESCE((_values->>'minimum_notice_minutes')::int, 1440),\n        COALESCE((_values->>'buffer_before_minutes')::int, 0), COALESCE((_values->>'buffer_after_minutes')::int, 0),\n        COALESCE((_values->>'window_business_days')::int, 6), COALESCE((_values->>'price_cents')::int, 0),\n        COALESCE(_values->>'currency', 'usd'), COALESCE((_values->>'requires_payment')::boolean, true),\n        COALESCE((_values->>'active')::boolean, true), (_values->>'schedule_id')::uuid\n      );\n    WHEN 'event.update' THEN\n      UPDATE public.booking_event_types SET\n        label = COALESCE(_values->>'label', label),\n        length_minutes = COALESCE((_values->>'length_minutes')::int, length_minutes),\n        slot_interval_minutes = CASE WHEN _values ? 'slot_interval_minutes' THEN (_values->>'slot_interval_minutes')::int ELSE slot_interval_minutes END,\n        minimum_notice_minutes = COALESCE((_values->>'minimum_notice_minutes')::int, minimum_notice_minutes),\n        buffer_before_minutes = COALESCE((_values->>'buffer_before_minutes')::int, buffer_before_minutes),\n        buffer_after_minutes = COALESCE((_values->>'buffer_after_minutes')::int, buffer_after_minutes),\n        window_business_days = COALESCE((_values->>'window_business_days')::int, window_business_days),\n        price_cents = COALESCE((_values->>'price_cents')::int, price_cents),\n        currency = COALESCE(_values->>'currency', currency),\n        requires_payment = COALESCE((_values->>'requires_payment')::boolean, requires_payment),\n        active = COALESCE((_values->>'active')::boolean, active),\n        schedule_id = COALESCE((_values->>'schedule_id')::uuid, schedule_id), updated_at = now()\n      WHERE slug = _action->>'slug';\n    WHEN 'event.deactivate' THEN\n      UPDATE public.booking_event_types SET active = false, updated_at = now() WHERE slug = _action->>'slug';\n    WHEN 'availability.replace' THEN\n      _schedule_id := (_action->>'schedule_id')::uuid;\n      DELETE FROM public.booking_availability WHERE schedule_id = _schedule_id;\n      INSERT INTO public.booking_availability (schedule_id, day_of_week, start_time, end_time)\n      SELECT _schedule_id, (r->>'day_of_week')::int, (r->>'start_time')::time, (r->>'end_time')::time\n      FROM jsonb_array_elements(COALESCE(_values->'ranges', '[]'::jsonb)) r;\n    WHEN 'override.upsert' THEN\n      INSERT INTO public.booking_date_overrides (schedule_id, local_date, closed, ranges, active)\n      VALUES ((_action->>'schedule_id')::uuid, (_action->>'local_date')::date,\n        COALESCE((_values->>'closed')::boolean, false), COALESCE(_values->'ranges', '[]'::jsonb), true)\n      ON CONFLICT (schedule_id, local_date) DO UPDATE SET closed = excluded.closed,\n        ranges = excluded.ranges, active = true, updated_at = now();\n    WHEN 'override.deactivate' THEN\n      UPDATE public.booking_date_overrides SET active = false, updated_at = now()\n      WHERE schedule_id = (_action->>'schedule_id')::uuid AND local_date = (_action->>'local_date')::date;\n    WHEN 'pass.create' THEN\n      INSERT INTO public.booking_passes (\n        student_id, student_email, student_name, token_hash, booking_slug,\n        total_credits, session_length_minutes, status, expires_at, notes, created_by\n      ) VALUES (\n        (_values->>'student_id')::uuid, _values->>'student_email', _values->>'student_name',\n        _values->>'token_hash', _values->>'booking_slug', (_values->>'total_credits')::int,\n        COALESCE((_values->>'session_length_minutes')::int, 70), 'active',\n        (_values->>'expires_at')::timestamptz, _values->>'notes', _actor_id\n      );\n    WHEN 'pass.update' THEN\n      UPDATE public.booking_passes SET status = COALESCE(_values->>'status', status),\n        expires_at = CASE WHEN _values ? 'expires_at' THEN (_values->>'expires_at')::timestamptz ELSE expires_at END,\n        notes = CASE WHEN _values ? 'notes' THEN _values->>'notes' ELSE notes END, updated_at = now()\n      WHERE id = (_action->>'id')::uuid;\n    WHEN 'pass.revoke' THEN\n      UPDATE public.booking_passes SET status = 'revoked', updated_at = now() WHERE id = (_action->>'id')::uuid;\n    WHEN 'booking.cancel' THEN\n      PERFORM public.cancel_booking_redemption((_action->>'id')::uuid, false, _actor_id);\n    WHEN 'booking.refund' THEN\n      PERFORM public.cancel_booking_redemption((_action->>'id')::uuid, true, _actor_id);\n    WHEN 'booking.reschedule' THEN\n      UPDATE public.booking_pass_redemptions SET start_at = (_values->>'start_at')::timestamptz,\n        end_at = (_values->>'end_at')::timestamptz, calendar_action = 'update', calendar_status = 'pending',\n        needs_gcal_sync = true, gcal_state = 'pending', updated_at = now()\n      WHERE id = (_action->>'id')::uuid AND status = 'booked';\n    ELSE\n      RAISE EXCEPTION 'invalid_scheduling_action';\n  END CASE;\n\n  _after := public.scheduling_action_before(_action);\n  UPDATE public.scheduling_previews SET used_at = now() WHERE token = _preview_token;\n  INSERT INTO public.scheduling_audit_log (actor_id, interface, device_name, kind, target, before_state, after_state)\n  VALUES (_actor_id, _interface, _device_name, _kind, _action - 'values' - 'kind', _before, _after)\n  RETURNING id INTO _audit_id;\n  RETURN jsonb_build_object('audit_id', _audit_id, 'before', _before, 'after', _after);\nEND;\n$$;\n\nCREATE FUNCTION public.undo_scheduling_audit(\n  _audit_id uuid, _actor_id uuid, _interface text, _device_name text\n) RETURNS jsonb LANGUAGE plpgsql SECURITY DEFINER SET search_path = public AS $$\nDECLARE\n  _original public.scheduling_audit_log%ROWTYPE;\n  _new_id uuid;\n  _current jsonb;\nBEGIN\n  SELECT * INTO _original FROM public.scheduling_audit_log WHERE id = _audit_id;\n  IF NOT FOUND THEN RAISE EXCEPTION 'audit_not_found'; END IF;\n  IF _original.undo_of IS NOT NULL OR EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM public.scheduling_audit_log WHERE undo_of = _audit_id) THEN\n    RAISE EXCEPTION 'audit_not_undoable';\n  END IF;\n  IF split_part(_original.kind, '.', 1) NOT IN ('event', 'availability', 'override', 'pass') THEN\n    RAISE EXCEPTION 'audit_not_undoable';\n  END IF;\n\n  IF _original.kind LIKE 'event.%' THEN\n    IF _original.before_state = 'null'::jsonb THEN\n      UPDATE public.booking_event_types SET active = false, updated_at = now() WHERE slug = _original.target->>'slug';\n    ELSE\n      UPDATE public.booking_event_types SET label = _original.before_state->>'label',\n        length_minutes = (_original.before_state->>'length_minutes')::int,\n        slot_interval_minutes = (_original.before_state->>'slot_interval_minutes')::int,\n        minimum_notice_minutes = (_original.before_state->>'minimum_notice_minutes')::int,\n        buffer_before_minutes = (_original.before_state->>'buffer_before_minutes')::int,\n        buffer_after_minutes = (_original.before_state->>'buffer_after_minutes')::int,\n        window_business_days = (_original.before_state->>'window_business_days')::int,\n        price_cents = (_original.before_state->>'price_cents')::int,\n        currency = _original.before_state->>'currency',\n        requires_payment = (_original.before_state->>'requires_payment')::boolean,\n        active = (_original.before_state->>'active')::boolean,\n        schedule_id = (_original.before_state->>'schedule_id')::uuid, updated_at = now()\n      WHERE slug = _original.target->>'slug';\n    END IF;\n  ELSIF _original.kind = 'availability.replace' THEN\n    DELETE FROM public.booking_availability WHERE schedule_id = (_original.target->>'schedule_id')::uuid;\n    INSERT INTO public.booking_availability (schedule_id, day_of_week, start_time, end_time)\n    SELECT (_original.target->>'schedule_id')::uuid, (r->>'day_of_week')::int,\n      (r->>'start_time')::time, (r->>'end_time')::time FROM jsonb_array_elements(_original.before_state) r;\n  ELSIF _original.kind LIKE 'override.%' THEN\n    IF _original.before_state = 'null'::jsonb THEN\n      DELETE FROM public.booking_date_overrides WHERE schedule_id = (_original.target->>'schedule_id')::uuid\n        AND local_date = (_original.target->>'local_date')::date;\n    ELSE\n      INSERT INTO public.booking_date_overrides (schedule_id, local_date, closed, ranges, active)\n      VALUES ((_original.before_state->>'schedule_id')::uuid, (_original.before_state->>'local_date')::date,\n        (_original.before_state->>'closed')::boolean, _original.before_state->'ranges',\n        (_original.before_state->>'active')::boolean)\n      ON CONFLICT (schedule_id, local_date) DO UPDATE SET closed = excluded.closed,\n        ranges = excluded.ranges, active = excluded.active, updated_at = now();\n    END IF;\n  ELSE\n    UPDATE public.booking_passes SET status = _original.before_state->>'status',\n      expires_at = (_original.before_state->>'expires_at')::timestamptz,\n      notes = _original.before_state->>'notes', updated_at = now()\n    WHERE id = (_original.target->>'id')::uuid;\n  END IF;\n\n  _current := CASE split_part(_original.kind, '.', 1)\n    WHEN 'event' THEN (SELECT to_jsonb(t) FROM public.booking_event_types t WHERE t.slug = _original.target->>'slug')\n    WHEN 'availability' THEN (SELECT COALESCE(jsonb_agg(to_jsonb(a) - 'id' - 'created_at' ORDER BY a.day_of_week, a.start_time), '[]'::jsonb) FROM public.booking_availability a WHERE a.schedule_id = (_original.target->>'schedule_id')::uuid)\n    WHEN 'override' THEN (SELECT to_jsonb(o) FROM public.booking_date_overrides o WHERE o.schedule_id = (_original.target->>'schedule_id')::uuid AND o.local_date = (_original.target->>'local_date')::date)\n    WHEN 'pass' THEN (SELECT to_jsonb(p) - 'token_hash' FROM public.booking_passes p WHERE p.id = (_original.target->>'id')::uuid)\n  END;\n  INSERT INTO public.scheduling_audit_log\n    (actor_id, interface, device_name, kind, target, before_state, after_state, undo_of)\n  VALUES (_actor_id, _interface, _device_name, 'undo.' || _original.kind, _original.target,\n    _original.after_state, COALESCE(_current, 'null'::jsonb), _original.id)\n  RETURNING id INTO _new_id;\n  RETURN jsonb_build_object('audit_id', _new_id, 'undo_of', _original.id, 'after', _current);\nEND;\n$$;\n\n-- A date override replaces weekly hours. Conflict checks remain in the claim RPC.\nCREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.is_single_session_slot_bookable(\n  _start_at timestamptz, _now timestamptz DEFAULT now(), _event_slug text DEFAULT 'single-session'\n) RETURNS boolean LANGUAGE plpgsql STABLE SET search_path = public AS $$\nDECLARE\n  _event public.booking_event_types%ROWTYPE;\n  _tz text;\n  _length int;\n  _pitch int;\n  _local_start timestamp;\n  _local_date date;\n  _local_time time;\n  _override public.booking_date_overrides%ROWTYPE;\nBEGIN\n  SELECT * INTO _event FROM public.booking_event_types WHERE slug = _event_slug AND active;\n  IF NOT FOUND THEN RETURN false; END IF;\n  SELECT timezone INTO _tz FROM public.booking_schedules WHERE id = _event.schedule_id;\n  _tz := COALESCE(_tz, 'America/Toronto');\n  _length := _event.length_minutes;\n  _pitch := COALESCE(_event.slot_interval_minutes, _length);\n  IF _start_at IS NULL OR _start_at < _now + make_interval(mins => _event.minimum_notice_minutes) THEN RETURN false; END IF;\n  _local_start := _start_at AT TIME ZONE _tz;\n  _local_date := _local_start::date;\n  _local_time := _local_start::time;\n  IF _local_date < (_now AT TIME ZONE _tz)::date OR _local_date > public.single_session_booking_window_end(_now, _event_slug) THEN RETURN false; END IF;\n  SELECT * INTO _override FROM public.booking_date_overrides\n  WHERE schedule_id = _event.schedule_id AND local_date = _local_date AND active;\n  IF FOUND THEN\n    IF _override.closed THEN RETURN false; END IF;\n    RETURN EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM jsonb_array_elements(_override.ranges) r\n      WHERE _local_time >= (r->>'start_time')::time\n        AND _local_time + make_interval(mins => _length) <= (r->>'end_time')::time\n        AND mod(floor(extract(epoch FROM (_local_time - (r->>'start_time')::time)) / 60)::int, _pitch) = 0);\n  END IF;\n  RETURN EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM public.booking_availability a\n    WHERE a.schedule_id = _event.schedule_id AND a.day_of_week = extract(dow FROM _local_date)::int\n      AND _local_time >= a.start_time AND _local_time + make_interval(mins => _length) <= a.end_time\n      AND mod(floor(extract(epoch FROM (_local_time - a.start_time)) / 60)::int, _pitch) = 0);\nEXCEPTION WHEN OTHERS THEN RETURN false;\nEND;\n$$;\n\nALTER TABLE public.booking_schedules ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;\nALTER TABLE public.booking_date_overrides ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;\nALTER TABLE public.scheduling_audit_log ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;\nALTER TABLE public.scheduling_previews ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;\n\nREVOKE ALL ON public.booking_schedules, public.booking_date_overrides,\n  public.scheduling_audit_log, public.scheduling_previews FROM public, anon, authenticated;\nGRANT ALL ON public.booking_schedules, public.booking_date_overrides,\n  public.scheduling_audit_log, public.scheduling_previews TO service_role;\nREVOKE ALL ON FUNCTION public.scheduling_preview(jsonb),\n  public.apply_scheduling_action(jsonb, uuid, uuid, text, text),\n  public.undo_scheduling_audit(uuid, uuid, text, text),\n  public.scheduling_action_is_allowed(text),\n  public.scheduling_action_before(jsonb) FROM public, anon, authenticated;\nGRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.scheduling_preview(jsonb),\n  public.apply_scheduling_action(jsonb, uuid, uuid, text, text),\n  public.undo_scheduling_audit(uuid, uuid, text, text) TO service_role;\n\r\n"}]
